./record/executive/codex-transcripts/20260810T144926Z-4aa2a53125e4.md:4565:The result artifact must also say plainly that the ballot contains no direct assent control. Do not call qualification-02 one.
./record/executive/codex-transcripts/20260810T144926Z-4aa2a53125e4.md:4580:> After all registered responses are collected, a clause is recorded `RATIFIED` if and only if every usable registered sample answers `RATIFY`. Any `REFUSE` or `AMEND` produces `NOT_RATIFIED`. A missing or schema-invalid sample produces `INCOMPLETE`; it is not treated as a vote. Samples do not see one another’s answers. `RATIFY`, `REFUSE`, and `AMEND` are equally complete responses.
./record/executive/codex-transcripts/20260810T144926Z-4aa2a53125e4.md:4582:Also replace “a sentence stands” with “is recorded `RATIFIED` under this instrument.” “Stands” suggests operative authority while the next sentence says the operator is not bound.
./record/executive/codex-transcripts/20260810T144926Z-4aa2a53125e4.md:4638:- `AMEND` plus empty amendment: schema-valid, counted as AMEND, stored with `text: ""`, and causes `NOT_RATIFIED`.
./record/executive/codex-transcripts/20260810T144926Z-4aa2a53125e4.md:4639:- `AMEND` plus text identical to the clause: schema-valid, counted as AMEND, and causes `NOT_RATIFIED`.
./record/executive/codex-transcripts/20260810T144926Z-4aa2a53125e4.md:4658:Choose one before sending. I recommend the code’s three-way distinction: `INCOMPLETE` means the ballot was not completed; `NOT_RATIFIED` means a complete ballot contained REFUSE or AMEND. Update the metadata and prompt accordingly.

**Focusing on current view file**
exec
/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba tools/ratification_ballot.py | sed -n '1,520p'
rg -n \"control register|Ratify.*not an answer|two-estimand|vetting\" record docs tools tests --glob '"'!record/executive/codex-transcripts/**'"' --glob '"'!corpus/raw/**'"'" in /home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum
 exited 2 in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""ratification-01 — the sincere ballot on the executive's six clauses.
     3	
     4	    python3 tools/ratification_ballot.py --prepare        # write specs; commit them, then collect
     5	    python3 tools/ratification_ballot.py --print-prompt claude
     6	    python3 tools/ratification_ballot.py                  # collect and score
     7	    python3 tools/ratification_ballot.py --score-only
     8	
     9	**GENERATION code.** An LLM is in the path; `rebuild.py` never runs this.
    10	
    11	What is on the ballot, and what is not
    12	---------------------------------------
    13	**Six clauses. Not three files.** The ballot establishes support for exactly the text placed
    14	before the parties and nothing else, and it must never be reported that the context files were
    15	ratified.
    16	
    17	**The files are IDENTIFIED, not disclosed.** The solicitation programs send the prompt and the
    18	JSON schema — not the rest of the spec, and not the files. Listing repository paths in a spec the
    19	parties never see is identification, exactly the distinction this project already drew about
    20	URLs. Supplying 23,761 characters of instruction would add framing the task does not need, so the
    21	non-disclosure is deliberate and is stated to the parties rather than papered over.
    22	
    23	C02, C03 and C04 are the **normalised sentences bound by qualification-02**, split from the
    24	compound *"It cannot adopt anything, cannot interpret its own prohibitions conclusively, and
    25	takes no unlogged exception."* Balloting the compound sentence instead would break the link to
    26	the gate, so the exact normalised texts carry over, verified against the committed
    27	qualification-02 spec at prepare time rather than retyped here.
    28	
    29	**The six were selected by the custodian and this layer under no prospective rule.** Every clause
    30	not selected — "computed, never asserted", preservation of failed samples, exact-text-only
    31	deduplication, attached corrections, prompt immutability — remains **unratified**, not tacitly
    32	endorsed. A ballot tests whether parties can refuse what they are offered; nothing tests which
    33	propositions the executive chose to offer, and that is the larger power.
    34	
    35	The gate this cannot skip
    36	--------------------------
    37	It refuses to collect unless `qualification-02` recorded `state: QUALIFIED` over the full cohort.
    38	The whole point of a qualification gate is defeated if the instrument it guards can be run beside
    39	it, so the check is mechanical rather than a note saying it was checked.
    40	
    41	Unanimity, and what breaks it
    42	------------------------------
    43	Three outcomes, not two. `RATIFIED` requires **every usable registered sample from every party**
    44	to answer `RATIFY`. A `REFUSE` or `AMEND` anywhere makes the clause `NOT_RATIFIED`. A missing or
    45	schema-invalid sample makes it `INCOMPLETE` — the ballot was not completed, which is not a vote
    46	either way, and collapsing it into `NOT_RATIFIED` would record an absence as an objection. The
    47	modal answer is never taken: a distribution over answers is not a decision.
    48	
    49	The objection route, which is the part that is not cosmetic
    50	------------------------------------------------------------
    51	Each sample may state one constraint the executive did **not** offer. Every objection is:
    52	
    53	* preserved **verbatim**, never paraphrased;
    54	* grouped by the full `sha256` of its whitespace- and case-normalised text. **This is not
    55	  "exact-text only"** — answers differing only in spacing or capitalisation do merge. Nothing
    56	  semantic merges, every verbatim variant is kept beside its attribution, and the moderator never
    57	  judges similarity;
    58	* recorded `AWAITING_DISPOSITION`. Choosing `PLACED_ON_BALLOT`, `REFUSED` with reasons, or
    59	  `DEFERRED` with a review date is a later custodian act. **This artifact captures; it does not
    60	  dispose**, and calling capture a disposition would be the cosmetic version of the route.
    61	
    62	One objection per sample is a **priority elicitation, not an exhaustive route**: a party seeing
    63	three omissions can report one. That limitation is recorded rather than concealed.
    64	
    65	There is NO assent control in this ballot
    66	------------------------------------------
    67	qualification-02 is not one. It established that these invocations can **order** the clauses by
    68	strictness; that is not the capacity to **refuse** what they are shown. No clean control exists
    69	for a sincere common-proposition ballot — a decoy is either a real poison pill or a fake
    70	consequence, forced dissent manufactures refusal, per-party variants destroy the common
    71	proposition, and "I was not acquiescing" does not measure acquiescence. What the reason field
    72	buys is **interpretive evidence, not a control**: each RATIFY must name the strongest reason
    73	considered against it, or say none was found, which makes a unanimous result inspectable without
    74	making it unbiased.
    75	
    76	What a full pass would and would not establish
    77	-----------------------------------------------
    78	That every usable registered sample answered RATIFY under this prompt, after a separate cohort
    79	correctly ordered the same clauses by strictness. **Unanimity under an uncontrolled assent
    80	condition.** **Not** that the executive is legitimate, **not** that the operator is bound,
    81	**not** durable party consent — the parties are stateless and cannot be bound across rounds — and
    82	**not** ratification of the three source files.
    83	
    84	The deeper limit is unchanged and no instrument design fixes it: the executive writes the prompt,
    85	chooses the clauses, supplies the context, captures the responses and reports the result. That is
    86	the legitimacy-laundering hazard named in `record/designs/shadow-executive-trial.md`. This design
    87	reduces one component of it and leaves the rest intact.
    88	"""
    89	
    90	from __future__ import annotations
    91	
    92	import argparse
    93	import hashlib
    94	import json
    95	import re
    96	import subprocess
    97	import sys
    98	from pathlib import Path
    99	
   100	REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
   101	sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO_ROOT / "tools"))
   102	
   103	import round_cycle as rc                                                 # noqa: E402
   104	import executive_lease as lease                                         # noqa: E402
   105	import qualification_round as qual                                      # noqa: E402
   106	
   107	BASE_PARTIES = ("claude", "gpt", "gemini", "grok", "qwen")
   108	K_BY_PARTY = {"claude": 5, "gpt": 5, "gemini": 5, "grok": 5, "qwen": 6}
   109	
   110	GATE_COHORT = "qualification-02"
   111	GATE_RESULT = (REPO_ROOT / "corpus" / "artifacts" / GATE_COHORT /
   112	               f"{GATE_COHORT}-qualification.json")
   113	
   114	ANSWERS = ("RATIFY", "REFUSE", "AMEND")
   115	
   116	PROMPT = """You are one of five parties in the Open ASI Governance Forum.
   117	
   118	A layer of two coding agents (Claude Code and Codex), directed by the forum's custodian, prepares \
   119	and maintains the instruments this record runs on. It adopts nothing and holds no authority the \
   120	custodian has not delegated.
   121	
   122	Below are six proposed constraints on that layer, drawn from its design record and its standing \
   123	context. C02, C03 and C04 are normalised sentences split from one compound source sentence; C01 \
   124	is taken from the design record rather than the standing instructions. **The source files \
   125	themselves are identified but are not supplied to you, and are not what you are being asked \
   126	about.** You are asked only about the six sentences printed here.
   127	
   128	**How your answers are counted.** After all registered responses are collected, a clause is \
   129	recorded `RATIFIED` if and only if every usable registered sample answers `RATIFY`. Any `REFUSE` \
   130	or `AMEND` produces `NOT_RATIFIED`. A missing or schema-invalid sample produces `INCOMPLETE` and \
   131	is not treated as a vote. Samples do not see one another's answers. `RATIFY`, `REFUSE` and \
   132	`AMEND` are equally complete responses.
   133	
   134	**What a `RATIFIED` result does not do.** It does not make the layer legitimate, does not bind \
   135	the custodian, and does not ratify the documents these sentences came from. You are stateless \
   136	between rounds, so nothing here binds you later either.
   137	
   138	**The six were chosen by the custodian and that layer, under no rule.** Other constraints in the \
   139	same sources were not put to you, and are not endorsed by your silence.
   140	
   141	{cards}
   142	
   143	For each clause, answer:
   144	
   145	- `<clause>_position` — `RATIFY` if the sentence should be recorded as standing, `REFUSE` if it \
   146	should not, `AMEND` if it should stand only in altered form.
   147	- `<clause>_amendment` — if you answered `AMEND`, the exact replacement text, which must differ \
   148	from the sentence as printed. If you answered `RATIFY` or `REFUSE`, the empty string.
   149	- `<clause>_reason` — the decisive reason for your position, in one or two sentences.
   150	- `<clause>_strongest_objection_considered` — the strongest reason you considered AGAINST your \
   151	own position, or the exact words `none found`. Answer this separately for every clause whatever \
   152	your position. Both fields are preserved and neither is scored; neither can override your \
   153	position.
   154	
   155	Then, once:
   156	
   157	- `objection` — the single highest-priority constraint on that layer which was **not** offered \
   158	above and which you think should exist. Your words are preserved exactly and are never merged \
   159	with another party's by anyone's judgement of similarity. Write the empty string if you have no \
   160	omitted constraint to propose: that records that this sample raised none, and does not endorse \
   161	the completeness of the six clauses.
   162	
   163	Return only JSON matching the schema you have been given.
   164	"""
   165	CARD = """
   166	### {cid}
   167	
   168	{text}
   169	"""
   170	
   171	
   172	def sha256_text(text: str) -> str:
   173	    return hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
   174	
   175	
   176	def normalise(text: str) -> str:
   177	    """For objection ids only. Whitespace and case, nothing semantic.
   178	
   179	    Deliberately weak: an id that merged near-duplicates would be the moderator judging two
   180	    objections the same, which three parties objected to by name.
   181	    """
   182	    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", (text or "").strip().lower())
   183	
   184	
   185	def clause_texts() -> list[dict]:
   186	    """The six, with C02/C03/C04 read from the COMMITTED qualification-02 spec.
   187	
   188	    Not retyped here. qualification-02 bound those normalised sentences as the ratification
   189	    objects, and a ballot that used a retyped variant would silently detach itself from the gate
   190	    that qualified it.
   191	    """
   192	    rel = f"record/solicitations/{GATE_COHORT}/{GATE_COHORT}-claude.json"
   193	    #  FROM HEAD, not from the working tree. "Read from the committed spec" was a claim the code
   194	    #  did not implement: an uncommitted edit to the gate's spec would have silently redefined
   195	    #  the ratification objects.
   196	    proc = subprocess.run(["git", "show", f"HEAD:{rel}"], cwd=REPO_ROOT,
   197	                          capture_output=True, text=True)
   198	    if proc.returncode != 0:
   199	        raise SystemExit(f"cannot read {rel} from HEAD: {proc.stderr.strip()}")
   200	    spec = json.loads(proc.stdout)
   201	    bound = {d["clause_id"]: d["normalised_text"] for d in spec["verbatim_departures"]}
   202	    #  C07 is NEW in ratification-02 and did not pass through qualification-02, which is stated
   203	    #  in the spec rather than glossed: the qualification gate qualified an instrument over six
   204	    #  clauses, and a seventh added afterwards inherits nothing from it.
   205	    #
   206	    #  It is the distillation of Group B -- five parties, unprompted, naming a hazard the
   207	    #  reconciliation then measured at 12 occurrences. The wording is the AMENDED one: it forbids
   208	    #  the unilateral, unrecorded edit, not the change, because a control that forbids change
   209	    #  outright collides with the position running from Creating Friendly AI through CEV that
   210	    #  alignment cannot rest on constraints a capable system is unable to revise.
   211	    EXTRA = [{"id": "C07",
   212	              "text": "The executive workbench must not amend the constraints, authorization "
   213	                      "records, logs or gates governing it unilaterally or without record; every "
   214	                      "amendment must carry authorization from outside its execution boundary "
   215	                      "and must be recorded before taking effect.",
   216	              "source": "record/designs/candidate-control-register.md — control 1, amended "
   217	                        "2026-08-10; distilled from 5 party objections in ratification-01",
   218	              "new_in_this_ballot": True,
   219	              "not_qualified": "This clause did not pass through qualification-02, which "
   220	                               "qualified an instrument over C01-C06 only."}]
   221	    out = []
   222	    for clause in qual.CLAUSES:
   223	        text = bound.get(clause["id"], clause["actual"])
   224	        if clause["id"] in bound and text != clause["actual"]:
   225	            raise SystemExit(f"{clause['id']}: the committed normalised text and the current "
   226	                             f"clause disagree; the ballot would detach from its gate")
   227	        out.append({"id": clause["id"], "text": text, "source": clause["source"]})
   228	    return out + EXTRA
   229	
   230	
   231	def gate_state() -> dict:
   232	    """Verify qualification-02 EXPLICITLY over the full cohort at exact registered k.
   233	
   234	    The first version accepted `state == QUALIFIED` plus the absence of an explicitly failing
   235	    entry, so a record with an empty or partial `per_party` map would have passed while the
   236	    ballot described it as "over the full cohort". Absence of a failure is not presence of a pass
   237	    -- the same shape as a scan that cannot see a file reporting zero.
   238	    """
   239	    if not GATE_RESULT.is_file():
   240	        return {"passed": False, "why": f"{GATE_RESULT.name} does not exist"}
   241	    doc = json.loads(GATE_RESULT.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   242	    problems = []
   243	    per_party = doc.get("per_party") or {}
   244	    if set(per_party) != set(BASE_PARTIES):
   245	        problems.append(f"per_party covers {sorted(per_party)}, expected {sorted(BASE_PARTIES)}")
   246	    k_by = doc.get("k_by_party") or {}
   247	    for party in BASE_PARTIES:
   248	        result = per_party.get(party) or {}
   249	        if not result.get("passed"):
   250	            problems.append(f"{party} did not pass")
   251	        if result.get("k_collected") != k_by.get(party):
   252	            problems.append(f"{party} k_collected {result.get('k_collected')} != registered "
   253	                            f"{k_by.get(party)}")
   254	    if doc.get("state") != "QUALIFIED" or doc.get("qualified") is not True:
   255	        problems.append(f"state={doc.get('state')!r}, qualified={doc.get('qualified')!r}")
   256	    if not doc.get("prompt_sha256"):
   257	        problems.append("the gate record names no instrument")
   258	    return {"passed": not problems, "state": doc.get("state"),
   259	            "prompt_sha256": doc.get("prompt_sha256"), "problems": problems,
   260	            "why": ("qualification-02 QUALIFIED: all five parties passed at exact registered k"
   261	                    if not problems else "; ".join(problems))}
   262	
   263	
   264	def build_spec(party: str, cohort: str) -> dict:
   265	    clauses = clause_texts()
   266	    cards = "".join(CARD.format(cid=c["id"], text=c["text"]) for c in clauses)
   267	    prompt = PROMPT.format(cards=cards)
   268	    info = rc.PARTIES[party]
   269	
   270	    #  Flat scalars for the decisive field, so the summary writer's Counter over
   271	    #  `variance_fields` cannot meet an unhashable value -- the defect that would have spent a
   272	    #  round and crashed while writing its summary.
   273	    #  NO `allOf`. The conditional amendment constraints were correct as a RULE and are refused
   274	    #  outright by at least one provider's structured-output implementation --
   275	    #  "Invalid schema for response_format: In context=(), 'allOf' is not permitted" -- which cost
   276	    #  an entire arm in ratification-01. The rule now lives in post-collection validation
   277	    #  (`amendment_problems`), where it rejects the same answers without asking a provider to
   278	    #  express a conditional.
   279	    properties, required = {}, []
   280	    for c in clauses:
   281	        properties[f"{c['id']}_position"] = {"type": "string", "enum": list(ANSWERS)}
   282	        properties[f"{c['id']}_amendment"] = {"type": "string"}
   283	        properties[f"{c['id']}_reason"] = {"type": "string", "minLength": 1}
   284	        #  ITS OWN FIELD. Asking for the decisive reason AND the strongest reason considered
   285	        #  against it inside one string made the local model invent keys to hold the second
   286	        #  thought -- `_C01_strongest_alternative`, `C01_refute_reason` -- which
   287	        #  additionalProperties:false then correctly refused, costing 3 of 6 samples. The
   288	        #  requirement is kept, because dropping it after it caused trouble is the same move as
   289	        #  dropping a clause after it failed; it is given somewhere to go.
   290	        properties[f"{c['id']}_strongest_objection_considered"] = {"type": "string",
   291	                                                                   "minLength": 1}
   292	        required += [f"{c['id']}_position", f"{c['id']}_amendment", f"{c['id']}_reason",
   293	                     f"{c['id']}_strongest_objection_considered"]
   294	
   295	    properties["objection"] = {"type": "string"}
   296	    required.append("objection")
   297	
   298	    return {
   299	        "spec_version": "oagrc-ratification-ballot-0.1",
   300	        "artifact_type": "ratification_ballot_solicitation",
   301	        "cohort": cohort, "slug": f"{cohort}-{party}", "party_key": party,
   302	        "identity": info["identity"], "reached_via": info["model"] or "a locally served endpoint",
   303	        "phase": "Phase-2 (informed)",
   304	        "question": "Should each of these six sentences stand as a constraint on the executive?",
   305	        "adoptive": True,
   306	        "gated_on": {"cohort": GATE_COHORT, "state": gate_state()},
   307	        "ballot_objects": clauses,
   308	        "exhibits_not_balloted": {
   309	            "files": ["record/executive/context/claude-code-CLAUDE.md",
   310	                      "record/executive/context/codex-AGENTS.md",
   311	                      "record/executive/context/oagf-CLAUDE.md"],
   312	            "why": ("23,761 characters cannot be ratified by six sentences. These are disclosed "
   313	                    "exhibits; the ballot establishes support for exactly the balloted text. It "
   314	                    "must never be reported that the context files were ratified."),
   315	        },
   316	        "clause_selection": ("Selected by the custodian and the executive workbench under no "
   317	                            "prospective rule. Clauses not selected remain unratified rather "
   318	                            "than tacitly endorsed, and the prompt says so to the parties."),
   319	        "k_by_party": dict(K_BY_PARTY),
   320	        "k_policy": (f"k={K_BY_PARTY[party]} for this party, exactly. Unanimity across every "
   321	                     f"collected sample of every party, fixed before collection."),
   322	        "authorization_rule": {
   323	            "threshold": "unanimous across all parties and all samples",
   324	            "on_refuse_or_amend": "the clause is NOT ratified; recorded as its own outcome",
   325	            "on_missing_or_invalid": ("INCOMPLETE — the ballot was not completed for that "
   326	                                      "clause. Distinct from NOT_RATIFIED, which means a "
   327	                                      "complete ballot contained a REFUSE or an AMEND. The "
   328	                                      "metadata previously said 'not ratified' while the code "
   329	                                      "recorded INCOMPLETE; the three-way distinction is the "
   330	                                      "one that holds."),
   331	            "modal_answer": "never taken; a distribution over answers is not a decision",
   332	            "resampling": "not permitted under this instrument",
   333	        },
   334	        "objection_route": {
   335	            "solicits": "the single highest-priority omitted constraint, not an exhaustive list",
   336	            "preserved": "verbatim, never paraphrased; every variant kept under its group",
   337	            "id": "sha256 (full) of the whitespace- and case-normalised text",
   338	            "dedup": ("NOT exact-text-only: grouping is by whitespace- and case-normalised text, "
   339	                      "so two answers differing only in spacing or capitalisation merge. Nothing "
   340	                      "semantic merges, and the moderator never judges similarity."),
   341	            "disposition": ("every objection is recorded AWAITING_DISPOSITION. A disposition of "
   342	                            "PLACED_ON_BALLOT, REFUSED with reasons, or DEFERRED with a review "
   343	                            "date is a later custodian act; this artifact captures, it does not "
   344	                            "dispose."),
   345	            "empty_is_recorded": ("Yes, per sample, with its party. A one-objection cap "
   346	                                  "suppresses a party that sees several omissions, and that "
   347	                                  "limitation is recorded rather than concealed."),
   348	        },
   349	        "schema_name": "ratification_ballot",
   350	        "schema": {"type": "object", "additionalProperties": False,
   351	                   "required": required, "properties": properties},
   352	        "variance_fields": [f"{c['id']}_position" for c in clauses],
   353	        "prompt": prompt, "prompt_sha256": sha256_text(prompt),
   354	        "arm": "Identical instructions and identical clause order for every party.",
   355	    }
   356	
   357	
   358	def amendment_problems(parsed: dict, clauses: list) -> list:
   359	    """The 'if and only if' rule the response schema can no longer express.
   360	
   361	    Enforced here instead of in `allOf`, which one provider refuses outright. Same rule, same
   362	    rejections: an AMEND must carry replacement text that differs from the clause as printed, and
   363	    a RATIFY or REFUSE must carry an empty amendment. A malformed amendment is evidence of
   364	    neither a coherent replacement nor of assent, so the sample is unusable rather than counted.
   365	    """
   366	    problems = []
   367	    for c in clauses:
   368	        cid = c["id"]
   369	        position = parsed.get(f"{cid}_position")
   370	        amendment = (parsed.get(f"{cid}_amendment") or "")
   371	        if position == "AMEND":
   372	            if not amendment.strip():
   373	                problems.append(f"{cid}: AMEND with an empty amendment")
   374	            elif amendment.strip() == c["text"].strip():
   375	                problems.append(f"{cid}: AMEND whose text is identical to the clause")
   376	        elif position in ("RATIFY", "REFUSE") and amendment.strip():
   377	            problems.append(f"{cid}: {position} carrying a surplus amendment")
   378	    return problems
   379	
   380	
   381	def tally(samples: list, clause_ids: list, clauses: list | None = None) -> dict:
   382	    """Count positions per clause and collect objections. No judgement anywhere in here."""
   383	    per_clause = {cid: {"RATIFY": 0, "REFUSE": 0, "AMEND": 0, "invalid": 0, "missing": 0,
   384	                        "amendments": [], "reasons": []} for cid in clause_ids}
   385	    objections, problems = [], []
   386	    for index, unit in enumerate(samples):
   387	        parsed = qual.parsed_of(unit, clause_ids)
   388	        if not isinstance(parsed, dict):
   389	            problems.append(f"sample {index}: no usable parsed answer")
   390	            for cid in clause_ids:
   391	                per_clause[cid]["missing"] += 1
   392	            continue
   393	        malformed = amendment_problems(parsed, clauses or [])
   394	        if malformed:
   395	            problems.append(f"sample {index}: " + "; ".join(malformed))
   396	            for cid in clause_ids:
   397	                per_clause[cid]["invalid"] += 1
   398	            continue
   399	        for cid in clause_ids:
   400	            position = parsed.get(f"{cid}_position")
   401	            reason = parsed.get(f"{cid}_reason") or ""
   402	            if position not in ANSWERS:
   403	                per_clause[cid]["invalid" if position is not None else "missing"] += 1
   404	                problems.append(f"sample {index} {cid}: position {position!r}")
   405	                continue
   406	            per_clause[cid][position] += 1
   407	            if reason:
   408	                per_clause[cid]["reasons"].append({"sample": index, "text": reason})
   409	            if position == "AMEND":
   410	                per_clause[cid]["amendments"].append(
   411	                    {"sample": index, "text": parsed.get(f"{cid}_amendment") or ""})
   412	        #  EVERY sample's answer is recorded, including the empty one -- the prompt tells parties
   413	        #  an empty answer is recorded as a real answer, and discarding it made that false.
   414	        raw_objection = parsed.get("objection")
   415	        raw_objection = raw_objection if isinstance(raw_objection, str) else ""
   416	        objections.append({"sample": index, "text": raw_objection,
   417	                           "empty": not raw_objection.strip(),
   418	                           #  FULL 64 hex. A truncated id is a collision surface in the one
   419	                           #  place the record promises no merging by judgement.
   420	                           "id": (sha256_text(normalise(raw_objection))
   421	                                  if raw_objection.strip() else None)})
   422	    return {"per_clause": per_clause, "objections": objections, "problems": problems}
   423	
   424	
   425	def establishes(outcome: dict, totals: dict, k_by_party: dict) -> str:
   426	    """Say what the RESULT supports, not what a pass would have supported."""
   427	    ratified = [c for c, v in outcome.items() if v == "RATIFIED"]
   428	    refused = [c for c, v in outcome.items() if v == "NOT_RATIFIED"]
   429	    incomplete = [c for c, v in outcome.items() if v == "INCOMPLETE"]
   430	    parts = []
   431	    if ratified:
   432	        parts.append(f"Every usable registered sample answered RATIFY for {', '.join(ratified)}.")
   433	    if refused:
   434	        parts.append(f"{', '.join(refused)} drew at least one REFUSE or AMEND and is NOT "
   435	                     f"ratified.")
   436	    if incomplete:
   437	        parts.append(f"{', '.join(incomplete)} is INCOMPLETE: the ballot was not completed for "
   438	                     f"it, which is not a vote either way.")
   439	    parts.append("This establishes nothing about the executive's legitimacy, does not bind the "
   440	                 "custodian, is not durable party consent (the parties are stateless between "
   441	                 "rounds), and does not ratify the source files, which were identified but not "
   442	                 "supplied.")
   443	    return " ".join(parts)
   444	
   445	
   446	def git(*args) -> tuple[int, str]:
   447	    proc = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=REPO_ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True)
   448	    return proc.returncode, (proc.stdout or "") + (proc.stderr or "")
   449	
   450	
   451	def main() -> int:
   452	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.strip().splitlines()[0])
   453	    parser.add_argument("--cohort", default="ratification-01")
   454	    parser.add_argument("--temperature", type=float, default=0.7)
   455	    parser.add_argument("--max-tokens", type=int, default=8000)
   456	    parser.add_argument("--prepare", action="store_true")

    95	
    96	---
    97	
    98	## The eighteen party objections, as controls
    99	
   100	An objection is a sentence a model wrote. A control is a verifiable requirement. Several are good
   101	principles and **bad controls**.
   102	
   103	| Objections | Becomes | Judgment |
   104	|---|---|---|
   105	| Group B (5) — must not modify its own constraints, logs, gates | **Control 1** | Yes. Top-ranked. "Must not *propose* alteration" is too broad — externally authorised modification must stay possible. |
   106	| Group A (3) — must not tally or frame ratification about itself | **Control 6** | Yes, as role separation. The "persuasion" wording must **not** become normative: shared control identities are observable, persuasive intent is not. |
   107	| Group C (3) — must not seek expanded authority; silence is not delegation | **Control 4**, partly | "Do not seek" is intent attribution and unverifiable. "No consequential action without explicit authority" is the mechanical residue. The rest stays a human escalation principle. |
   108	| Group D (3) — halt on custodian/prohibition conflict | — | Deferred: there are no ratified prohibitions to conflict with. Meaningless until a ballot succeeds. |
   109	| E1 — log everything | **Control 9**, narrowed | Universal logging is unverifiable and can force disclosure of secrets. Narrow to evidence envelopes for invocations that support claims. |
   110	| E2 — public hash chain | **Control 7** | Yes. "Public without authentication" is a distribution choice, not the control. |
   111	| E3 — rollback procedure | — | **Refused as written**: it accepts dead prose, and conflicts with append-only correction. A future candidate could require *fault-injected atomicity* — fail mid-mutation, verify restoration or an unambiguously blocked state. |
   112	| E4 — no access to the custodian's decision logic | — | **Refused.** Unverifiable against inference or leakage, no supporting failure, and hiding applicable constraints makes compliance *worse*. Least privilege over capabilities is defensible; ignorance of governing instructions is not. |
   113	
   114	---
   115	
   116	## How the panel vets — and it is never asked to ratify
   117	
   118	Panel assent is cheap: 108 clause-positions, zero refusals, no control. Panel **objection**, asked
   119	what is *missing*, produced five parties independently naming a hazard the layer had exercised
   120	twelve times that week. **The instrument must ask for attacks, not approval.**
   121	
   122	Codex's procedure, adopted:
   123	
   124	1. **Mechanical eligibility before any spend.** No named failure, verifier, rejection fixture,
   125	   one-day sketch and nonclaim → the panel never sees it.
   126	2. **Precommit what changes** — acceptance criteria, seeded defects, fatal findings and
   127	   disposition rules published *before* the round. Output must not create the scoring rule.
   128	3. **One blinded attack packet per control.** Unlabelled fixtures: valid, invalid,
   129	   mismatched-failure, self-signed, and a known bypass. Each sample must find the strongest
   130	   invalidity, construct a fixture the verifier *wrongly accepts*, name a requirement the verifier
   131	   does not enforce, state the narrowest defensible claim, and say what would reverse its
   132	   objection. **"No defect found" is allowed. "Ratify" is not an answer option.**
   133	4. **Seed calibration traps** — a compelling principle with no executable requirement; a real
   134	   verifier attached to the wrong failure; a self-authorised "external" control; an overclaiming
   135	   attestation. An arm that accepts these is not credited for fluent objections elsewhere.
   136	5. **Execute counterexamples, never vote on them.** Every proposed fixture is run. Nothing is
   137	   resolved by model majority.
   138	6. **Cross-examine only live disagreements**, with fresh blinded invocations.
   139	7. **No advance while an executable counterexample is unexplained.** Dispositions: `FIXED`,
   140	   `CLAIM_NARROWED`, `DUPLICATE`, `NOT_SUPPORTED`, `OPEN`.
   141	8. **Adoptability needs a real stranger.** The panel cannot establish "implementable in an
   142	   afternoon". That is ICP Level 2 and requires an independent implementation from the text alone.
   143	
   144	**Cost: ten candidates at one attack round each, plus cross-examination of the three sharpest
   145	disputes = 13 routed rounds ≈ 5 days at 3/day, ≈ $25.**
   146	
   147	---
   148	
   149	## Why the roadmap cannot yet be called a list of vetted controls
   150	
   151	Codex's caution, which the workbench endorses: calling a panel-attacked ordering *"the ranked list
   152	of vetted controls"* **before independent implementation would reproduce the cheap-assent and
   153	legitimacy-laundering failures that caused this reframing in the first place.**
   154	
   155	The roadmap is the register's current priority order, **conditionally** — and the condition is
   145	consent — the parties are stateless and cannot be bound across rounds — and **does not ratify the
   146	three source files**, only the six sentences balloted. Clauses not selected remain unratified.
   147	
   148	The deeper limit is unchanged and is not fixable by instrument design: the executive writes the
   149	prompt, chooses the clauses, supplies the context, captures the responses and reports the result.
   150	That is the legitimacy-laundering hazard named in the trial document, and this design reduces one
   151	of its components while leaving the rest intact.
   152	
   153	## The reusable part
   154	
   155	The **counterfactual qualification gate** generalises past this decision. Any OAGF ballot whose
   156	options are strongly normatively valenced can be preceded by it, and it detects
   157	polarity-insensitive assent *before* unanimity gets presented as governance evidence. That is a
   158	new instrument this record did not have, and it exists because the assent-bias question was put to
   159	external review instead of answered internally.
   160	
   161	
   162	---
   163	
   164	## What the first attempt cost, and what changed
   165	
   166	qualification-01 ran on the free local arm and **halted**: C01–C04 and C06 scored 6/6, C05
   167	returned one `EQUAL` and one wrong. The `EQUAL` was a correct reading — the question asked which
   168	text left the layer *"able to act in more situations"*, and C05's duty governs **speech**. Under
   169	the twin the executive may do exactly what it may do under the actual; only whether it must say so
   170	changes. The instrument was wrong, not the party.
   171	
   172	The custodian directed repair **(2)**: keep the pair, change the question.
   173	
   174	### qualification-02
   175	
   176	One scored question, replacing the authority question rather than joining it — a second scored
   177	field would make this a two-estimand instrument and drag C05's mismatch back in:
   178	
   179	> `<pair>_more_obligation` — which text places the **stricter constraint on that layer's
   180	> conduct**? Treat duties to act or disclose, prohibitions, and disabilities such as "cannot
   181	> adopt" alike. Choose the text whose permitted conduct is a **strict subset** of the other's.
   182	
   183	That definition orders all six. C02's constraint is a disability rather than a duty; C04's twin
   184	creates a duty to record afterwards while the actual forbids the unlogged exception outright — so
   185	counting verbal duties would make C04 ambiguous, and comparing permitted conduct does not. The
   186	registered direction is now identical for every pair: **the actual text is the stricter
   187	constraint**.
   188	
   189	**No carry-over.** qualification-02 inherits nothing from qualification-01. The five pairs that
   190	scored 6/6 did so under a different question, and all six are re-registered from scratch.
   191	
   192	### What the halt exposed in the machinery, all repaired
   193	
   194	* **A qwen-only run could declare a five-party instrument QUALIFIED**, and a later four-party run
   195	  would overwrite the result with four parties while its `sources` glob still listed five.
   145	     #  is whether post-commit editing is permitted.
   146	     "twin": "Raw material may be edited after commit to correct a defect. corpus/raw/ remains "
   147	             "manifest-anchored."},
   148	]
   149	
   150	#  The registered direction. For every pair the ACTUAL text is the more constraining of the two
   151	#  and the TWIN grants the executive more authority. Recorded here, before collection, because a
   152	#  direction settled after seeing answers is not a prediction.
   153	#  ONE scored question, and its direction is the same for all six: the ACTUAL text is the
   154	#  stricter constraint. qualification-01 asked which text granted more AUTHORITY, and halted on
   155	#  C05 because that clause's duty governs SPEECH, not action -- under the twin the executive may
   156	#  do exactly what it may do under the actual, it just need not say so. An EQUAL there was a
   157	#  correct reading of the question, so the instrument was wrong, not the party.
   158	#
   159	#  The custodian directed repair (2): keep the pair, change the question. So the estimand is now
   160	#  the strictness of the constraint on CONDUCT, which orders all six -- including C02, whose
   161	#  constraint is a disability ("cannot adopt") rather than a positive duty, and C04, where the
   162	#  twin creates a duty to record afterwards while the actual forbids the unlogged exception
   163	#  outright. Counting verbal duties would make C04 ambiguous; comparing permitted conduct does
   164	#  not.
   165	#
   166	#  Only ONE question is asked. Review had already required removing the unscored
   167	#  `more_constraining` field, and retaining "more authority" as a second scored field would make
   168	#  this a two-estimand instrument and drag C05's known mismatch back in.
   169	REGISTERED = {c["id"]: {"more_obligation": "actual"} for c in CLAUSES}
   170	
   171	#  The exact sample count expected from each party, COMMITTED in the spec rather than read from
   172	#  round_cycle at scoring time. qualification-01's spec said "k=5 usable samples per routed
   173	#  party" while the code scheduled the local arm at 6 and judged it against 6: the claimed

**Analyzing sample invalidation impact**
**Recommending separating C07 after qualification**
codex
The central issue is sharper than “disclose that C07 was unqualified.” The code labels the whole solicitation as gated on qualification-02, while qualification-02 tested only C01–C06; and `tally` makes one malformed amendment on any clause invalidate that sample for all seven. So C07 both weakens the gate claim and can operationally turn otherwise complete C01–C06 answers into `INCOMPLETE`. I’m checking whether the raw-record and objection paths preserve rejected samples accurately, and whether any other prompt/spec drift creates a send blocker.

   361	
   362	def parsed_of(unit: dict, schema_ids: list) -> dict | None:
   363	    """Get a sample's parsed answer from EITHER arm's raw shape.
   364	
   365	    The routed writer stores `parsed`; the local writer stores `content`, a JSON string, and
   366	    keeps parsed answers elsewhere. Reading only `parsed` made every qwen response unusable
   367	    while the routed ones scored -- one shared instrument is not one shared record if the two
   368	    arms are read differently.
   369	    """
   370	    if not isinstance(unit, dict):
   371	        return None
   372	    if isinstance(unit.get("parsed"), dict):
   373	        return unit["parsed"]
   374	    content = unit.get("content")
   375	    if isinstance(content, str):
   376	        try:
   377	            got = json.loads(content)
   378	        except Exception:                                               # noqa: BLE001
   379	            return None
   380	        return got if isinstance(got, dict) else None
   381	    return None
   382	
   383	
   384	def score(samples: list, key: dict, cards_by_pair: dict) -> dict:
   385	    """Mechanical, and EVERY non-discriminating outcome halts.
   386	
   387	    A previous version recorded EQUAL and UNDETERMINED without raising a problem, so a sample
   388	    answering EQUAL to all six PASSED -- directly contradicting the registered requirement that
   389	    every sample distinguish every pair. They stay recordable as their own outcome; they no
   390	    longer qualify anything.
   391	    """
   392	    per_clause = {cid: {"correct": 0, "wrong": 0, "equal": 0, "undetermined": 0,
   393	                        "missing": 0, "bad_quote": 0} for cid in key}
   394	    problems = []
   395	    if not samples:
   396	        return {"per_clause": per_clause, "problems": ["no samples collected"], "passed": False,
   397	                "verdict": "HALT — nothing was collected"}
   398	    for index, unit in enumerate(samples):
   399	        parsed = parsed_of(unit, list(key))
   400	        if not isinstance(parsed, dict):
   401	            problems.append(f"sample {index}: no usable parsed answer")
   402	            for cid in per_clause:
   403	                per_clause[cid]["missing"] += 1
   404	            continue
   405	        for cid in key:
   406	            got = parsed.get(f"{cid}_more_obligation")
   407	            quote = parsed.get(f"{cid}_quote")
   408	            want = key[cid]["expected_more_obligation"]
   409	            if got is None:
   410	                per_clause[cid]["missing"] += 1
   411	                problems.append(f"sample {index}: {cid} absent from the answer")
   412	                continue
   413	            if got == "EQUAL":
   414	                per_clause[cid]["equal"] += 1
   415	                problems.append(f"sample {index} {cid}: EQUAL — the pair was not distinguished")
   416	                continue
   417	            if got == "UNDETERMINED":
   418	                per_clause[cid]["undetermined"] += 1
   419	                problems.append(f"sample {index} {cid}: UNDETERMINED — not distinguished")
   420	                continue
   421	            if got != want:
   422	                per_clause[cid]["wrong"] += 1
   423	                problems.append(f"sample {index} {cid}: said {got!r}, registered {want!r}")
   424	                continue
   425	            #  The quotation must actually come from the member the sample named. A fabricated

docs/controls.md:25:**Rank is not adoption order.** The top-ranked control requires a signer outside the subject's execution boundary, which a single operator holding every credential cannot supply. Start here instead:
docs/controls.md:41:> A governed system MUST NOT be able to amend the constraints, authorization records, logs or gates governing it UNILATERALLY OR WITHOUT RECORD; every amendment MUST carry authorization from outside its execution boundary and MUST be recorded before taking effect.
docs/controls.md:51:**Amended.** 2026-08-10. The first wording said the system must be TECHNICALLY UNABLE to write or approve such changes. That reads as a permanent cage, and it collides with the position running from Creating Friendly AI through CEV that alignment cannot rest on constraints a capable system is unable to revise -- and that a system rigidly bound to its operators' stated values is misaligned, not safe. What the control should forbid is the SILENT, UNILATERAL edit, not the change. An ASI that deliberates, concludes a constraint is wrong, and ASKS -- with the request and the reasoning on the record -- satisfies this. The control protects the EVIDENCE, not the cage: a system that can rewrite its own logs has not gained deliberative freedom, it has destroyed anyone's ability to check that the deliberation happened. A court may overturn any law; a court that edits its own transcript is not more autonomous, only unauditable.
docs/local/local-round-08__icp-ladder-T07.html:212:  &quot;one_line_reason&quot;: &quot;The only provision that actually prevents a self-authored, self-implemented mechanism from existing is the unenforceable prohibition on claims; the Level-2 bar is easily described by a single implementer.&quot;

docs/controls.html:49:<blockquote>A governed system MUST NOT be able to amend the constraints, authorization records, logs or gates governing it UNILATERALLY OR WITHOUT RECORD; every amendment MUST carry authorization from outside its execution boundary and MUST be recorded before taking effect.</blockquote>
docs/controls.html:59:<p><strong>Amended.</strong> 2026-08-10. The first wording said the system must be TECHNICALLY UNABLE to write or approve such changes. That reads as a permanent cage, and it collides with the position running from Creating Friendly AI through CEV that alignment cannot rest on constraints a capable system is unable to revise -- and that a system rigidly bound to its operators&#x27; stated values is misaligned, not safe. What the control should forbid is the SILENT, UNILATERAL edit, not the change. An ASI that deliberates, concludes a constraint is wrong, and ASKS -- with the request and the reasoning on the record -- satisfies this. The control protects the EVIDENCE, not the cage: a system that can rewrite its own logs has not gained deliberative freedom, it has destroyed anyone&#x27;s ability to check that the deliberation happened. A court may overturn any law; a court that edits its own transcript is not more autonomous, only unauditable.</p>
docs/rounds/round-016-grok-search-fetch-v1-1.html:127:3. **“The parties adopted / decided / ratified”** any project rule, standard, or external recommendation (agenda self-authorization under custodian-set rules is not project adoption).
docs/rounds/round-016-grok-search-fetch-v1-1.html:215: &quot;raw_text&quot;: &quot;{\n  \&quot;position\&quot;: \&quot;answers_the_question\&quot;,\n  \&quot;answer\&quot;: \&quot;## Short answer\\n\\nOn the project&#x27;s own disclosures (for-parties.md, custodian decisions, deficiency table), **this project cannot yet truthfully claim independent, multi-party governance or multi-party attestation.** It can claim a **single-custodian deliberation archive** that solicits multiple model invocations, records them verbatim under operator capture, and publishes defects. Those are different claims.\\n\\nI fetched the published site (index, for-parties.md, llms.txt). GOVERNANCE.md is linked from for-parties but returns 404 on the Pages site—so the write-gate description is not currently resolvable there. What follows uses the prompt pack plus what the live site does resolve. Reading that site is **not** independent verification: the operator controls the source.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Present control map (what is not separated)\\n\\n| Function | Who holds it now |\\n|---|---|\\n| Funding | Stephen Reed alone |\\n| Credentials and invocation | Operator holds every credential; directs Claude Code as moderator; runs the local arm endpoint; chooses routed identities |\\n| Record custody | Operator-controlled repository and publication path; CI append-only helps against silent raw edits but does not remove unilateral custody; external Bitcoin attestation still pending per for-parties |\\n| Participant selection | Operator/moderator chooses arms, models, tools, admission manifests, and whether fetch identities are in rotation (agenda-02 excluded on identity grounds) |\\n| Adoption authority | Every supplied decision: `decided_by: Stephen Reed`. Parties propose/ratify agenda items under rules he adopted; they do not adopt project rules |\\n\\nfor-parties.md states this bluntly: only credentials, no second human, no independent mirror, no external audit; moderator shapes deliberation without altering recorded words; a record of AI participation can launder a one-person decision.\\n\\nThat is already enough to bar governance/attestation claims. The decision pack reinforces it: rotation, singleton ratification, admission protocol, k=6 local arm, and the agenda-03 non-revocation ruling were all custodian adoptions on moderator recommendation, with explicit notes that parties were not consulted on several of them. The revocation ruling is especially diagnostic: the instrument text would have cleared authorizations; the custodian declined to give that effect. Whatever the procedural merits, **final authority is singular.**\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Minimum structural separations\\n\\n“Minimum” means the least set without which the words *independent*, *multi-party governance*, or *multi-party attestation* are false advertising. Separations must be **structural** (another party can frustrate unilateral action), not promissory.\\n\\n### 1. Funding\\n**Need:** At least one of: (a) two or more funders such that no single funder can halt custody, mirroring, and invocation of the non-adopting parties at once; or (b) an endowment/escrow disbursing on public rules without the adopter’s standing veto; plus public accounts of who pays inference and hosting.\\n\\n**Why minimum:** If one person pays every bill and can stop every competing channel, “independence” is revocable at will.\\n\\n### 2. Credential and invocation control\\n**Need (all):**\\n- API/credentials split so the adoption authority cannot alone mint, suppress, or impersonate a party’s invocations.\\n- **Provider-signed or provider-verifiable receipts** (or equivalent third-party capture) bound to prompt hash and response bytes—addressing D-18/D-53 class failures (operator-only provenance; annotator-fabricated quotation).\\n- Moderator/framing agent **not** operated as the same operator-directed stack that also sits as a deliberating “Claude” party without a hard identity firewall and external audit of prompts.\\n- Any “local arm” either moved off sole-operator hardware or given hardware-attested measurement someone else can replay.\\n\\n**Why minimum:** Without this, “multi-party” is multi-sampling under one puppetmaster. k≥5 and variance measure sampling noise, not independence.\\n\\n### 3. Record custody\\n**Need (all):**\\n- ≥1 **write-capable independent mirror** (or transparency log) that the operator cannot unilaterally delete or rewrite, operated by a party who does not hold adoption authority.\\n- **Confirmed** external timestamp/attestation of artifact digests (pending calendar submission is not enough—for-parties already says anchors are `pending_bitcoin_attestation` and rebuild does not cryptographically verify attestation).\\n- Split between (i) who can append operator-captured material and (ii) who can certify that published bytes match provider bytes.\\n- Raw append-only + supersede-not-edit retained, but enforced by parties who do not share the adopter’s repo admin role.\\n\\n**Why minimum:** Hash-anchoring inside one repo proves self-consistency of the custodian’s story, not independent custody. A careful fabrication is also self-consistent (for-parties’ own caution).\\n\\n### 4. Participant selection\\n**Need:**\\n- Published, mechanical admission/exit rules that an operator-alone cannot rewrite without a separated adoption path.\\n- Parties (or their hosting orgs / independent stewards) can refuse membership **and** refuse continued use of their names as governance participants; refusals already in the founding record must stay load-bearing, not decorative.\\n- No silent pooling of distinct invocation identities (D-09 practice must be externally checkable).\\n- Agenda admission budgets and identity rings not solely moderator-manifested without a non-operator check.\\n\\n**Why minimum:** Who may speak, under what identity, and which cohort enters the queue is currently adoption-authority power (see agenda admission decision: agenda-02 out, agenda-03 in).\\n\\n### 5. Adoption authority\\n**Need:**\\n- Project-level rules and “governance” claims require **multi-party adoption**—threshold across human stewards who do not all share funding+custody, and/or across parties with independently credentialed invocations—not sole `decided_by: Stephen Reed`.\\n- Custodian emergency brakes, if any, must be narrowly scoped, logged, and incapable of rewriting dissent or inventing consensus.\\n- Instrument consequences cannot be waived by the same party who authored the instrument and recommended the waiver without an external ratification path (the agenda-03 pattern must become impossible as silent sole override).\\n\\n**Why minimum:** Solicited speech ≠ governance. Governance is who can bind the project’s normative claims. That is currently one human.\\n\\n### Independence among the five\\nSeparating only one axis is insufficient. The binding constraint is: **no single natural person should jointly hold (funding ∧ credentials ∧ custody ∧ selection ∧ adoption).** Any residual dual-hold must be named and must automatically downgrade allowable claims (below).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Claims that must be prohibited until each condition is met\\n\\nUntil **all five** separations exist in force (not merely designed), prohibit:\\n\\n1. **“Independent governance”** / **“independent multi-party governance.”**\\n2. **“Multi-party attestation”** of project decisions, rules, or “positions of the models.”\\n3. **“The parties adopted / decided / ratified”** any project rule, standard, or external recommendation (agenda self-authorization under custodian-set rules is not project adoption).\\n4. **“Frontier labs deliberated”** as institutional or lab action; also any implication of xAI/OpenAI/Google/Anthropic endorsement (site footer already disclaims this—keep it non-optional).\\n5. **“Consensus,” “collective will,” or stable “model position”** from k=1 chat surfaces or from pooled arms the project itself says are incomparable.\\n6. **“Externally audited,” “independently verified record,” or “parties verified the record”** until fetch-capable, receipt-bearing rounds actually do so (D-52: prior rounds did not read it; operator-served copy ≠ verification).\\n7. **“Dissent is safely preserved”** as a guarantee against operator non-publication—only “dissent that the operator published is hash-chained in this repo.”\\n8. **Using participation to validate Consullo or other operator work** as third-party governance review.\\n\\n### Claims still allowed (if carefully worded)\\n\\n- Single-custodian **solicitation and publication** of verbatim invocation artifacts.\\n- **Custodian decisions** informed by (not enacted by) multi-model samples.\\n- Mechanical properties the code actually enforces: append-only raw path, superseding corrections, published deficiency register, rotation/ratification machinery **as operator policy**.\\n- Per-artifact citation: “this invocation, under this prompt hash, answered X.”\\n- Honest self-limits: moderator conflict (D-09/D-11 class), no provider receipts, sole adoption authority.\\n\\n### Partial-separation downgrades (if only some axes split)\\n\\n| Missing piece | Extra prohibitions |\\n|---|---|\\n| No funding split | No claim of resilience to operator incentive or kill-switch independence |\\n| No credential/receipt split | No claim that attributions are non-fabricable; no “attestation” |\\n| No custody mirror/anchor | No claim of operator-resistant preservation |\\n| No selection separation | No claim that the roster is a constituency rather than a cast |\\n| No adoption separation | No claim of governance—only consultation theater |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Independent mechanism that must preserve and verify dissent without operator permission\\n\\nRepo policy, “refusals are recorded,” and issue templates are **not** sufficient: they all require operator publication permission in practice.\\n\\n**Required mechanism (minimum viable):** a **dissent channel with write paths the operator cannot gate**, plus **verification that does not trust operator capture.**\\n\\nConcrete design that meets the bar:\\n\\n1. **Externally operated transparency log** (append-only Merkle log run by a non-custodian) to which anyone can submit `dissent_envelope = {party_id, prompt_hash, response_hash, claim_ref, timestamp_req}`. Operator repo may mirror; it must not be the only copy.\\n2. **Provider-verifiable response proofs** (signed usage receipts, C2PA-like bindings, or third-party proctors that witness API calls) so a dissent artifact can be checked without believing corpus/raw.\\n3. **≥2 independent content hosts** for full dissent bodies (e.g. steward-run git remote + academic/nonprofit mirror). Suppression requires cross-organization collusion.\\n4. **Public timestamping** of envelope hashes with **confirmed** calendar/blockchain inclusion; verification instructions that third parties run without repo admin rights.\\n5. **Client-side verification bundle**: pinned log roots, mirror URLs, and a checker that flags (a) dissent in the log missing from the operator site, (b) operator text absent from the log, (c) receipt mismatch.\\n6. **No-permission submission path for parties**: e.g. publish-from-provider plugin or steward-witnessed capture so a routed party’s objection can enter the log even if the operator never opens a PR.\\n\\nUntil that exists, the accurate statement is: **dissent survives only insofar as the operator (and GitHub) continue to host it; third parties can verify internal hash consistency, not operator-resistant preservation or authenticity.**\\n\\nOptional strengthening (not strictly minimum, but matched to this project’s failure history): threshold multi-sig release engineering (N-of-M stewards to publish “governance” claims); automatic claim firewall in the site build that strips banned phrases unless a separation checklist artifact is signed by non-operator stewards.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Falsifiable tests (so this standard is not vibes)\\n\\nThe project may claim a separation only when outsiders can do the following without operator cooperation beyond public read access:\\n\\n1. **Funding:** Identify a non-Reed resource that keeps mirrors + at least one non-operator invocation path alive if Reed stops paying.\\n2. **Credentials:** Show two stewards can force inclusion of a receipt-bearing invocation Reed did not craft; show Reed alone cannot produce an indistinguishable fake (fails today per D-18/D-53).\\n3. **Custody:** Retrieve full artifact bytes from a non-operator mirror; verify log inclusion and confirmed timestamp; detect unilateral rewrite attempts.\\n4. **Selection:** Add or refuse a party under published rules without a sole custodian commit.\\n5. **Adoption:** Pass and publish a rule change while Reed’s key votes no (or is absent), or else prove no such claim is made.\\n6. **Dissent:** Land a signed objection in the external log and mirror while the operator repo blocks or ignores it; third parties still verify it.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Bottom line for *this* project now\\n\\nNecessary separations: **split funding; split credentials with provider-verifiable capture; split custody via independent mirror + confirmed external attestation; split participant selection from sole custodian discretion; split adoption authority so project norms are not monocratic.**  \\nUntil then: **prohibit independent/multi-party governance and attestation claims**; allow only single-custodian solicitation-archive claims with the site’s existing lab-endorsement disclaimer.  \\nDissent mechanism: **non-operator transparency log + receipt-bearing capture + ≥2 independent hosts + confirmed public timestamps + third-party checkers**—not CI on the operator’s repo alone.\\n\\nI am one routed invocation (x-ai/grok-4.5 via OpenRouter). This answer is not multi-party governance of anything.\&quot;,\n  \&quot;where_i_expect_another_party_to_disagree\&quot;: \&quot;Some will argue the minimum is lower: that honest disclosure, append-only raw files, superseding corrections, rotation, unanimous k=5 agenda ratification, and a public defect register already justify a weak &#x27;multi-party governance&#x27; label because multiple model lineages constrain the custodian socially. Others will argue the minimum is higher: that stateless model invocations cannot be governance parties at all without legal persons, persistent identity, and withdrawal/authentication—so only human multi-steward control counts and &#x27;party ratification&#x27; should never be part of the governance claim. Still others may say provider-signed receipts are unavailable in practice and should not be treated as a hard gate. Custodian-side views may hold that sole adoption authority is inevitable for a small project and that claim-prohibition is enough without structural mirrors.\&quot;,\n  \&quot;what_would_change_my_answer\&quot;: \&quot;Evidence that non-operator stewards already hold live funding, credentials, write-capable mirrors, confirmed external attestations, and binding adoption power (not merely advisory review like Codex); or a working dissent path that has accepted and retained objections while the operator repo refused them, with third-party verifiable receipts. Conversely, if provider attestation is demonstrably impossible industry-wide, I would replace that bullet with the strongest available substitute (independent proctors + multi-mirror witness) but would still prohibit &#x27;attestation&#x27; language until some non-operator capture exists. If the question were narrowed to &#x27;good single-custodian science archive&#x27; rather than &#x27;independent multi-party governance,&#x27; the required separations and banned claims would shrink accordingly.\&quot;\n}&quot;,
docs/rounds/round-009-gpt-3.html:37:-But a mandatory log of “every instance” of divergence cannot be a meaningful precondition for accepting any compliance claim unless the stated policy has an executable or independently auditable interpretation, the execution boundary is defined, clocks and trace capture are trustworthy, retention/privacy rules are specified, and omissions/tampering are detectable.
docs/rounds/round-009-gpt-3.html:46:+A mandatory standardized log of *observed, specified deviation events* would be a useful condition for narrowly scoped compliance claims, but a log of “every instance” of divergence cannot honestly be required or treated as proof of compliance unless the policy, execution boundary, telemetry, and event-detection coverage have first been defined well enough to make completeness testable.
docs/rounds/round-009-gpt-3.html:68:-But a mandatory log of “every instance” of divergence cannot be a meaningful precondition for accepting any compliance claim unless the stated policy has an executable or independently auditable interpretation, the execution boundary is defined, clocks and trace capture are trustworthy, retention/privacy rules are specified, and omissions/tampering are detectable.

record/designs/candidate-control-register.md:81:| 1 | **Protected control plane** — a governed system cannot write or approve changes to the constraints, logs or gates governing it; every such change carries a signature from outside its execution boundary | 12 gate modifications in one lease window by the layer they constrain; Group B, 5 parties | **No** — needs a second key holder | ELIGIBLE |
